The earlier Lemma 3.8 checkpoint completed all four clauses of Lemma 3.8. For (c,N_F)=1 the formula is 𝒮_{q,c}(u)=C_{q₂}(u) q₁c² χ(q₁c) ξ_{q₁}(adj(A)u) R_{q₁c}(F*(u)). On the dual cone F*(u)=0 it is 𝒮_{q,c}(u)=C_{q₂}(u) q₁² χ(q₁) ξ_{q₁}(adj(A)u) S_c(u) for every positive c, including arbitrary bad-prime factors. The proofs use actual Ramanujan CRT, the zero-frequency unit count, φ(dc)=dφ(c) for d∣c, literal gcd/quotient arithmetic, and prime-power induction over actual complete sums. An actual supplied Dirichlet character is retained: agreement with the Jacobi coefficient at good composite moduli is proved from its stated good-prime Legendre values. The right-hand weight is the literal product of the original supplied prime functions. The bad/good modulus split is constructed, its gcd preservation is proved, and S_{c_bad}(u) is multiplied through both CRT identities without division, including when it vanishes. Both modulus-one boundaries are included. N≥1 is unused and is not inferred from (q,N)=1. Primitivity, the nonsquare condition, and the weight's zero value, norm bound and Fourier stratification bounds are also unnecessary for these identities. The completed Lemma 3.3 proves the bound for the actual complete sum: |S_c(u)|≤4√|det A| c³ for every positive modulus and every integral frequency, assuming only symmetry and integer nonsingularity of the source matrix. Translation and composite-modulus additive orthogonality give the squared-norm bound by c⁴ times the actual residue kernel cardinality of 2A. Surjective coordinate reduction and the finite-endomorphism index theorem show that this cardinality divides the integer range index. Mathlib's proved determinant-index theorem, based on Smith normal form, identifies that index with |det(2A)|=16|det A|. The square-root and actual unit-count bounds then give the exact source constant. Modulus one, even moduli, and arbitrary bad-prime factors are included; no good-prime, oddness, nonzero-frequency, or nonsquare-determinant hypothesis is imposed. ## Status convention

- **Pending:** no accepted complete export for the frozen statement.
- **Active:** currently being formalized; no completion is implied.
- **Partial:** explicitly identified clauses have checked exports; remaining clauses are listed.
- **Certified:** all clauses have kernel-checked proofs relative to registered literature inputs, and the statement and its dependencies have passed correspondence review.

A successful build does not change these statuses. Imported literature theorems remain explicit inputs; applying them and verifying their hypotheses are manuscript proof obligations. A generic supporting identity is not counted as a numbered result.

## Correspondence reviews for certified numbered results

### Lemma 2.1

[Statement correspondence review](certification/correspondence/delta_symbol.md)

Complete Lemma 2.1 relative to the separately registered Heath-Brown construction input EXT.HB_DELTA. The premise concerns the actual fixed source bump, sampling sum and kernel; it supplies their elementary source properties, the unnormalized identity T(Q,n)=Q*S(Q)*1_(n=0), and the raw sampling error for Q>1. It does not assume the manuscript-specific normalized statement or uniformity down to Q=3. The compact interval [7/12,11/12] and an explicitly rounded sample give one positive denominator bound throughout Q>=3. Raw order j+1 gives the integer-order normalization error, and a natural ceiling supplies every real j>=1. Actual residue phases, modulus one, all integer frequencies, and the finite support cutoff are proved internally. Smoothness is scoped Lean order infinity (the coercion of ENat.top), not the outer analytic top order. No normalized formula at Q=2 or uniform normalized bound as Q approaches 2 is imported. Twenty generic new exports use no literature premise; the nine source-specific exports use only the one registered HB input. All earlier completed claims and four earlier literature declarations are preserved.

### Lemma 2.2

[Statement correspondence review](certification/correspondence/delta_integrals.md)

Complete Lemma 2.2 relative to separately registered precise HB localization and analytic inputs and the separately attributed general Simon graph area formula. The original fixed kernel from Lemma 2.1 is retained. No final manuscript estimate is assumed as a premise: actual bump normalization, pieces and reconstruction, coordinate/operator derivative equivalence, annular gradient bound, actual Hessian nondegeneracy, root consistency, surface normalization and integrability, orthogonal transport, finite-family constants, r/v rescaling and all-positive-scale extension are internal. Five new HB declarations and one general area declaration are separately inspected. The stronger real-matrix endpoint needs only symmetry/nonsingularity; the nonsquare global determinant condition is unnecessary here. The absolute convergence obligation for arbitrary weights meeting the cone vertex remains separately Pending.

The subsequent weighted dual-cone consequence aux:conecount remains separately Pending and is not implemented in this checkpoint.

### Lemma 2.4

[Statement correspondence review](certification/correspondence/polynomial_box.md)

Complete Lemma 2.4 with the exact constant C(n,E)=E+...+E^n, independent of the coefficients, modulus, intervals, and Y. Actual multivariate polynomials are sliced in the first variable. A fixed nonzero coefficient polynomial at each prime detects every identically zero fibre and retains the total-degree bound. Actual polynomial roots, distinct-prime CRT uniqueness, and quotient blocks prove the one-dimensional interval estimate. The weighted expansion over subsets of degenerate primes is the squarefree version of the manuscript totient divisor sum. Its two product bounds and C^k+1<=(1+C)^k for k>=1 retain the exact dimension recurrence E*(1+C). The induction includes the empty prime set, m=1, n=1, empty intervals, nonzero constant polynomials, all degenerate fibres, and degrees at least the characteristic. The source wrapper uses independent polynomials only on the prime-divisor subtype and arbitrary order-connected real intervals with finite integer preimages of cardinality at most Y. The real-interval solution set is proved equal to the finite endpoint representation, and its finiteness is proved before its natural cardinality is used. No common nonvanishing direction, common polynomial, residue-distribution estimate, or degeneracy-count premise remains. The 49 new public theorem exports in nine modules use only standard logical foundations: 46 use Classical.choice, Quot.sound and propext, one uses propext alone, and two are axiom-free. No literature premise is added or used. All earlier numbered results and all 33 separately tracked supplementary obligations retain their statuses; the subsequent epsilon absorption is not included in this milestone.

### Lemma 3.1

[Statement correspondence review](certification/correspondence/complete_sum_crt.md)

Complete Lemma 3.1 for the actual source sums. Integer-lift independence, all scaled CRT substitutions and phase coefficients, the two local unit permutations, and the source prime-weight domain are proved explicitly. No coprimality between q and c is imposed. Modulus-one identities, multiplicativity of S_c, the squarefree prime-factor formula for C_q, and the bridge to the existing prime Fourier sum are separately checked supporting exports. Squarefreeness is unnecessary for the two main identities of the product-defined extension; it is retained for the prime-factor corollary. All 66 new exports use only standard foundations; no literature premise is used. The main analytic theorems remain Pending.

### Lemma 3.2

[Statement correspondence review](certification/correspondence/prime_power_gauss.md)

Complete Lemma 3.2 for a general symmetric integral matrix, every prime outside the literal N_F=2*abs(det A), every k>=1, and every integral frequency. The scalar condition p not dividing a is the source condition. The good-prime hypothesis supplies oddness, determinant nonsingularity, and all required prime-power units. The prime calculation constructs its orthogonal basis and determinant-square identity; actual residue-digit bijections and additive orthogonality prove V_(k+2)=p^4*V_k, including V_0=1. Square completion uses the actual adjugate and native residue-ring inverse. Unit reindexing gives the Ramanujan formula; affine orthogonality and the existing projective-ray equivalence give the point count. The pure and shifted integral formulas extend to k=0, while the unit-lift and final source conjunction retain k>=1. No diagonalization, Gauss-value, recurrence, or counting assumption survives in the endpoint. All 54 new exports use only standard logical foundations and no literature premise. The global analytic theorems remain Pending.

### Lemma 3.3

[Statement correspondence review](certification/correspondence/bad_prime_bound.md)

Complete Lemma 3.3 for the actual symmetric nonsingular integral quaternary matrix. The modulus is any nonzero natural number, representing every source integer c>=1; modulus one and all bad-prime factors are included. The allowed dependencies are the standard logical foundations `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. Individual exports may additionally use only the exact literature premises named in their ledger entry. Their actual Lean types and axiom dependencies are preserved in the [execution records](CHECKPOINT.md). 12 external Lean premises are registered separately from these proved exports; see [Trusted inputs](TRUSTED_INPUTS.md).
